Give Claude, Cursor, and other MCP clients powerful\\ncontext graph-based memory with temporal awareness.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003c/div\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eGraphiti is a framework for building and querying temporal context graphs for AI agents. Unlike static knowledge graphs,\\nGraphiti's context graphs track how facts change over time, maintain provenance to source data, and support both\\nprescribed and learned ontology — making them purpose-built for agents operating on evolving, real-world data.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eUnlike traditional retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) methods, Graphiti continuously integrates user interactions,\\nstructured and unstructured enterprise data, and external information into a coherent, queryable graph. The framework\\nsupports incremental data updates, efficient retrieval, and precise historical queries without requiring complete graph\\nrecomputation, making it suitable for developing interactive, context-aware AI applications.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eUse Graphiti to:\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cul dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eBuild context graphs that evolve with every interaction — tracking what's true now and what was true before.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eGive agents rich, structured context instead of flat document chunks or raw chat history.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003eQuery across time, meaning, and relationships with hybrid retrieval (semantic + keyword + graph traversal).\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003c/ul\\u003e\\n\\u003cp 2.83Z\\\"\\u003e\\u003c/path\\u003e\\u003c/svg\\u003e\\u003c/a\\u003e\\u003c/div\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eA \\u003cstrong\\u003econtext graph\\u003c/strong\\u003e is a temporal graph of entities, relationships, and facts — like \\u003cem\\u003e\\\"Kendra loves Adidas shoes (as of\\nMarch 2026).\\\"\\u003c/em\\u003e Unlike traditional knowledge graphs, each fact in a context graph has a validity window: when it became\\ntrue, and when (if ever) it was superseded. Entities evolve over time with updated summaries. Everything traces back to\\n\\u003cstrong\\u003eepisodes\\u003c/strong\\u003e — the raw data that produced it.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eWhat makes Graphiti unique is its ability to autonomously build context graphs from unstructured and structured data,\\nhandling changing relationships while preserving full temporal history.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eA context graph contains:\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cmarkdown-accessiblity-table\\u003e\\u003ctable\\u003e\\n\\u003cthead\\u003e\\n\\u003ctr\\u003e\\n\\u003cth\\u003eComponent\\u003c/th\\u003e\\n\\u003cth\\u003eWhat it stores\\u003c/th\\u003e\\n\\u003c/tr\\u003e\\n\\u003c/thead\\u003e\\n\\u003ctbody\\u003e\\n\\u003ctr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003e\\u003cstrong\\u003eEntities\\u003c/strong\\u003e (nodes)\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003ePeople, products, policies, concepts — with summaries that evolve over time\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003c/tr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003e\\u003cstrong\\u003eFacts / Relationships\\u003c/strong\\u003e (edges)\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003eTriplets (Entity → Relationship → Entity) with temporal validity windows\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003c/tr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctr\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003e\\u003cstrong\\u003eEpisodes\\u003c/strong\\u003e (provenance)\\u003c/td\\u003e\\n\\u003ctd\\u003eRaw data as ingested — the ground truth stream. processing and static data summarization, making them inefficient for\\nfrequently changing data. Graphiti addresses these challenges by providing:\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cul dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003e\\u003cstrong\\u003eTemporal Fact Management:\\u003c/strong\\u003e Facts have validity windows. When information changes, old facts are\\ninvalidated — not deleted. Query what's true now, or what was true at any point in time.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003e\\u003cstrong\\u003eEpisodes \\u0026amp; Provenance:\\u003c/strong\\u003e Every entity and relationship traces back to the episodes (raw data) that produced it.\\nFull lineage from derived fact to source.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003e\\u003cstrong\\u003ePrescribed \\u0026amp; Learned Ontology:\\u003c/strong\\u003e Define entity and edge types upfront via Pydantic models (prescribed), or let\\nstructure emerge from your data (learned). Start simple, evolve as patterns appear.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003e\\u003cstrong\\u003eIncremental Graph Construction:\\u003c/strong\\u003e New data integrates immediately without batch recomputation. The graph evolves\\nin real-time as episodes are ingested.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003e\\u003cstrong\\u003eHybrid Retrieval:\\u003c/strong\\u003e Combines semantic embeddings, keyword (BM25), and graph traversal for low-latency,\\nhigh-precision queries without reliance on LLM summarization.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003e\\u003cstrong\\u003eScalability:\\u003c/strong\\u003e Efficiently manages large datasets with parallel processing, pluggable graph backends, suitable\\nfor enterprise workloads.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003c/ul\\u003e\\n\\u003cp align=\\\"center\\\" Local servers are ideal for privacy-focused applications or avoiding API costs. The example below uses Ollama;\\nfor any other provider, point \\u003ccode\\u003ebase_url\\u003c/code\\u003e at its endpoint and set the appropriate \\u003ccode\\u003eapi_key\\u003c/code\\u003e and \\u003ccode\\u003emodel\\u003c/code\\u003e.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\u003cstrong\\u003eNote:\\u003c/strong\\u003e Use \\u003ccode\\u003eOpenAIGenericClient\\u003c/code\\u003e (not \\u003ccode\\u003eOpenAIClient\\u003c/code\\u003e) for these endpoints. It is optimized for local models with a\\nhigher default max token limit (16K vs 8K) and handles structured outputs across compatible providers.\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cp for entity/edge extraction and deduplication, and works best with models\\nand providers that reliably honor it (OpenAI, Anthropic, Gemini). Reliability varies across OpenAI-compatible providers and\\nespecially on smaller or local models, so \\u003ccode\\u003eOpenAIGenericClient\\u003c/code\\u003e exposes a \\u003ccode\\u003estructured_output_mode\\u003c/code\\u003e:\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cul dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003e\\u003ccode\\u003e\\\"json_schema\\\"\\u003c/code\\u003e (default): requests native structured output via \\u003ccode\\u003eresponse_format\\u003c/code\\u003e. Best on capable models and\\nproviders that enforce the schema via constrained decoding.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003e\\u003ccode\\u003e\\\"json_object\\\"\\u003c/code\\u003e: requests plain-JSON mode and injects the schema into the prompt instead. Use this for\\nproviders/models that don't reliably honor \\u003ccode\\u003ejson_schema\\u003c/code\\u003e — including some local servers that accept the \\u003ccode\\u003ejson_schema\\u003c/code\\u003e\\nrequest but don't actually constrain output to it, where \\u003ccode\\u003ejson_object\\u003c/code\\u003e can be \\u003cem\\u003emore\\u003c/em\\u003e reliable.\\u003c/li\\u003e\\n\\u003c/ul\\u003e\\n\\u003cp dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003eWhen using smaller or local models:\\u003c/p\\u003e\\n\\u003cul dir=\\\"auto\\\"\\u003e\\n\\u003cli\\u003ePrefer the most capable model you can run. diagnostics) (#1567)\n\n* ci(mcp): fail loudly when a same-repo run collects no tests\n\nThe exit-5-as-success path treated &quot;no tests collected&quot; as success\nunconditionally, so a future refactor that renamed the `integration` marker or\nthe test file would yield a green build that tested nothing — silently, on main\nand every PR. Gate the success path on OPENAI_API_KEY being absent (the fork\nself-skip case); when the key is present FalkorDB is already up, so exit 5 means\na broken test selection and now fails the job. Mirrors the same fix on the\nserver-tests workflow.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 (1M context) &lt;noreply@anthropic.com&gt;\n\n* ci: clarify the exit-5 failure diagnostic in both live-test workflows\n\nAddress review feedback: the key-present exit-5 path can also be reached when\nFalkorDB becomes unreachable (the suite has a second module-level skip via a\nhost-side TCP check, distinct from the in-container readiness gate), not only a\nbroken test selection. Broaden the failure message and comment to name both\ncauses, and stop conflating a renamed test file (a pytest usage error -&gt; exit 4,\ncaught separately) with a renamed/removed `integration` marker (the only case\nthat actually routes through the exit-5 branch). Applied to both\nmcp-server-tests.yml and server-tests.yml for consistency. (backslash-underscore), but\nvalidate_group_id (added to harden search against injection) rejects\nbackslashes. As a result, add_episode/search with the default group_id\nfailed with GroupIdValidationError once search re-validated the group_ids,\nbreaking the FalkorDB quickstart out of the box.\n\nUse a clean default group_id of '_' (passes validation) and escape\nnon-alphanumeric characters in group_ids when building RediSearch fulltext\nqueries, since RediSearch treats '_' and '-' as token separators/operators\n(an unescaped '_' produces a fulltext syntax error). class=\"react-directory-row-commit-cell\"><div><div class=\"react-directory-commit-message\"><a data-pjax=\"true\" title=\"fix(falkordb): make default group_id valid and escape it for RediSearch (#1549)\n\nThe FalkorDB default group_id was '\\_' (backslash-underscore), but\nvalidate_group_id (added to harden search against injection) rejects\nbackslashes. As a result, add_episode/search with the default group_id\nfailed with GroupIdValidationError once search re-validated the group_ids,\nbreaking the FalkorDB quickstart out of the box.\n\nUse a clean default group_id of '_' (passes validation) and escape\nnon-alphanumeric characters in group_ids when building RediSearch fulltext\nqueries, since RediSearch treats '_' and '-' as token separators/operators\n(an unescaped '_' produces a fulltext syntax error). The escaping is applied\nin both FalkorDriver.build_fulltext_query (active path) and the driver-ops\n_build_falkor_fulltext_query.\n\nVerified by running examples/quickstart/quickstart_falkordb.py end-to-end\nwith the default group_id (add episodes + hybrid search) against FalkorDB.\n\nCo-authored-by: Claude Opus 4.8 (1M context) &lt;noreply@anthropic.com&gt;\" class=\"Link--secondary\" (#1548)\n\n* deprecate(kuzu): mark Kuzu backend deprecated and drop it from the default test matrix\n\nThe upstream Kuzu project is no longer maintained, so the Kuzu backend is deprecated\nand will be removed in a future release. Users should migrate to Neo4j or FalkorDB.\n\n- KuzuDriver.__init__ now emits a DeprecationWarning.\n- Tests no longer parametrize over Kuzu by default (helpers_test.py): KUZU is opt-in via\n  ENABLE_KUZU while the driver still ships. This also removes the KUZU-only\n  community-clustering case that #1456's label_propagation rewrite trips.\n- README: deprecation admonitions on the install + usage sections; flagged in requirements.\n- pyproject: note the `kuzu` extra is deprecated.\n\nThe driver itself is unchanged and still functional; this is deprecation, not removal.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 (1M context) &lt;noreply@anthropic.com&gt;\n\n* docs: note Kuzu deprecation in AGENTS.md and CONTRIBUTING.md\n\nKeep contributor/agent docs consistent with the README deprecation: AGENTS.md no\nlonger lists Kuzu as a first-class backend, and CONTRIBUTING.md drops kuzu_driver.py\nfrom the recommended reference implementations (noting it's deprecated).\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 (1M context) Star this repo!</em></p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">&nbsp;</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-alert markdown-alert-tip\" dir=\"auto\"><p class=\"markdown-alert-title\" dir=\"auto\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-light-bulb mr-2\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"M8 1.5c-2.363 0-4 1.69-4 3.75 0 .984.424 1.625.984 2.304l.214.253c.223.264.47.556.673.848.284.411.537.896.621 1.49a.75.75 0 0 1-1.484.211c-.04-.282-.163-.547-.37-.847a8.456 8.456 0 0 0-.542-.68c-.084-.1-.173-.205-.268-.32C3.201 7.75 2.5 6.766 2.5 5.25 2.5 2.31 4.863 0 8 0s5.5 2.31 5.5 5.25c0 1.516-.701 2.5-1.328 3.259-.095.115-.184.22-.268.319-.207.245-.383.453-.541.681-.208.3-.33.565-.37.847a.751.751 0 0 1-1.485-.212c.084-.593.337-1.078.621-1.489.203-.292.45-.584.673-.848.075-.088.147-.173.213-.253.561-.679.985-1.32.985-2.304 0-2.06-1.637-3.75-4-3.75ZM5.75 12h4.5a.75.75 0 0 1 0 1.5h-4.5a.75.75 0 0 1 0-1.5ZM6 15.25a.75.75 0 0 1 .75-.75h2.5a.75.75 0 0 1 0 1.5h-2.5a.75.75 0 0 1-.75-.75Z\"></path></svg>Tip</p><p dir=\"auto\">Check out the new <a href=\"/getzep/graphiti/blob/main/mcp_server/README.md\">MCP server for Graphiti</a>! Give Claude, Cursor, and other MCP clients powerful\ncontext graph-based memory with temporal awareness.</p>\n</div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Graphiti is a framework for building and querying temporal context graphs for AI agents. Unlike static knowledge graphs,\nGraphiti's context graphs track how facts change over time, maintain provenance to source data, and support both\nprescribed and learned ontology — making them purpose-built for agents operating on evolving, real-world data.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Unlike traditional retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) methods, Graphiti continuously integrates user interactions,\nstructured and unstructured enterprise data, and external information into a coherent, queryable graph. The framework\nsupports incremental data updates, efficient retrieval, and precise historical queries without requiring complete graph\nrecomputation, making it suitable for developing interactive, context-aware AI applications.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Use Graphiti to:</p>\n<ul dir=\"auto\">\n<li>Build context graphs that evolve with every interaction — tracking what's true now and what was true before.</li>\n<li>Give agents rich, structured context instead of flat document chunks or raw chat history.</li>\n<li>Query across time, meaning, and relationships with hybrid retrieval (semantic + keyword + graph traversal).</li>\n</ul>\n<p dir=\"auto\">&nbsp;</p>\n<p align=\"center\" dir=\"auto\">\n    <animated-image data-catalyst=\"\" style=\"width: 700px;\"><a target=\"_blank\"       </svg>\n          </a>\n        </span>\n      </span></animated-image>\n</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">&nbsp;</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">What is a Context Graph?</h2><a id=\"user-content-what-is-a-context-graph\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: What is a Context Graph?\" href=\"#what-is-a-context-graph\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">A <strong>context graph</strong> is a temporal graph of entities, relationships, and facts — like <em>\"Kendra loves Adidas shoes (as of\nMarch 2026).\"</em> Unlike traditional knowledge graphs, each fact in a context graph has a validity window: when it became\ntrue, and when (if ever) it was superseded. Entities evolve over time with updated summaries. Everything traces back to\n<strong>episodes</strong> — the raw data that produced it.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">What makes Graphiti unique is its ability to autonomously build context graphs from unstructured and structured data,\nhandling changing relationships while preserving full temporal history.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">A context graph contains:</p>\n<markdown-accessiblity-table data-catalyst=\"\"><table>\n<thead>\n<tr>\n<th>Component</th>\n<th>What it stores</th>\n</tr>\n</thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Entities</strong> (nodes)</td>\n<td>People, products, policies, concepts — with summaries that evolve over time</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Facts / Relationships</strong> (edges)</td>\n<td>Triplets (Entity → Relationship → Entity) with temporal validity windows</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Episodes</strong> (provenance)</td>\n<td>Raw data as ingested — the ground truth stream. Every derived fact traces back here</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Custom Types</strong> (ontology)</td>\n<td>Developer-defined entity and edge types via Pydantic models</td>\n</tr>\n</tbody>\n</table></markdown-accessiblity-table>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Graphiti and Zep</h2><a id=\"user-content-graphiti-and-zep\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Graphiti and Zep\" href=\"#graphiti-and-zep\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Graphiti is the open-source temporal context graph engine at the core of\n<a href=\"https://www.getzep.com\" rel=\"nofollow\">Zep's</a> context infrastructure for AI agents. Zep manages context graphs at scale, providing\ngoverned, low-latency context retrieval and assembly for production agent deployments.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Using Graphiti, we've demonstrated Zep is\nthe <a href=\"https://blog.getzep.com/state-of-the-art-agent-memory/\" rel=\"nofollow\">State of the Art in Agent Memory</a>.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Read our paper: <a href=\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.13956\" rel=\"nofollow\">Zep: A Temporal Knowledge Graph Architecture for Agent Memory</a>.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">We're excited to open-source Graphiti, believing its potential as a context graph engine reaches far beyond memory\napplications.</p>\n<p align=\"center\" dir=\"auto\">\n    <a href=\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.13956\" rel=\"nofollow\"><img src=\"/getzep/graphiti/raw/main/images/arxiv-screenshot.png\" alt=\"Zep: A Temporal Knowledge Graph Architecture for Agent Memory\" width=\"700px\" style=\"max-width: 100%;\"></a>\n</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Zep vs Graphiti</h2><a id=\"user-content-zep-vs-graphiti\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Zep vs Graphiti\" href=\"#zep-vs-graphiti\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<markdown-accessiblity-table data-catalyst=\"\"><table>\n<thead>\n<tr>\n<th>Aspect</th>\n<th>Zep</th>\n<th>Graphiti</th>\n</tr>\n</thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>What they are</strong></td>\n<td>Managed context graph infrastructure for AI agents</td>\n<td>Open-source temporal context graph engine</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Context graphs</strong></td>\n<td>Manages vast numbers of per-user/entity context graphs with governance</td>\n<td>Build and query individual context graphs</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>User &amp; conversation management</strong></td>\n<td>Built-in users, threads, and message storage</td>\n<td>Build your own</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Retrieval &amp; performance</strong></td>\n<td>Pre-configured, production-ready retrieval with sub-200ms performance at scale</td>\n<td>Custom implementation required; performance depends on your setup</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Developer tools</strong></td>\n<td>Dashboard with graph visualization, debug logs, API logs; SDKs for Python, TypeScript, and Go</td>\n<td>Build your own tools</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Enterprise features</strong></td>\n<td>SLAs, support, security guarantees</td>\n<td>Self-managed</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Deployment</strong></td>\n<td>Fully managed or in your cloud</td>\n<td>Self-hosted only</td>\n</tr>\n</tbody>\n</table></markdown-accessiblity-table>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">When to choose which</h3><a id=\"user-content-when-to-choose-which\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: When to choose which\" href=\"#when-to-choose-which\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\"><strong>Choose Zep</strong> if you want a turnkey, enterprise-grade platform with security, performance, and support baked in.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\"><strong>Choose Graphiti</strong> if you want a flexible OSS core and you're comfortable building/operating the surrounding system.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Why Graphiti?</h2><a id=\"user-content-why-graphiti\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Why Graphiti?\" href=\"#why-graphiti\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Traditional RAG approaches often rely on batch processing and static data summarization, making them inefficient for\nfrequently changing data. Graphiti addresses these challenges by providing:</p>\n<ul dir=\"auto\">\n<li><strong>Temporal Fact Management:</strong> Facts have validity windows. When information changes, old facts are\ninvalidated — not deleted. Query what's true now, or what was true at any point in time.</li>\n<li><strong>Episodes &amp; Provenance:</strong> Every entity and relationship traces back to the episodes (raw data) that produced it.\nFull lineage from derived fact to source.</li>\n<li><strong>Prescribed &amp; Learned Ontology:</strong> Define entity and edge types upfront via Pydantic models (prescribed), or let\nstructure emerge from your data (learned). Start simple, evolve as patterns appear.</li>\n<li><strong>Incremental Graph Construction:</strong> New data integrates immediately without batch recomputation. The graph evolves\nin real-time as episodes are ingested.</li>\n<li><strong>Hybrid Retrieval:</strong> Combines semantic embeddings, keyword (BM25), and graph traversal for low-latency,\nhigh-precision queries without reliance on LLM summarization.</li>\n<li><strong>Scalability:</strong> Efficiently manages large datasets with parallel processing, pluggable graph backends, suitable\nfor enterprise workloads.</li>\n</ul>\n<p align=\"center\" dir=\"auto\">\n    <animated-image data-catalyst=\"\" style=\"width: 700px;\"><a target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener </span></animated-image>\n</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Graphiti vs. GraphRAG</h2><a id=\"user-content-graphiti-vs-graphrag\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Graphiti vs. GraphRAG\" href=\"#graphiti-vs-graphrag\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<markdown-accessiblity-table data-catalyst=\"\"><table>\n<thead>\n<tr>\n<th>Aspect</th>\n<th>GraphRAG</th>\n<th>Graphiti</th>\n</tr>\n</thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Primary Use</strong></td>\n<td>Static document summarization</td>\n<td>Dynamic, evolving context for agents</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Data Handling</strong></td>\n<td>Batch-oriented processing</td>\n<td>Continuous, incremental updates</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Knowledge Structure</strong></td>\n<td>Entity clusters &amp; community summaries</td>\n<td>Temporal context graph — entities, facts with validity windows, episodes, communities</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Retrieval Method</strong></td>\n<td>Sequential LLM summarization</td>\n<td>Hybrid semantic, keyword, and graph-based search</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Adaptability</strong></td>\n<td>Low</td>\n<td>High</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Temporal Handling</strong></td>\n<td>Basic timestamp tracking</td>\n<td>Explicit bi-temporal tracking with automatic fact invalidation</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Contradiction Handling</strong></td>\n<td>LLM-driven summarization judgments</td>\n<td>Automatic fact invalidation with temporal history preserved</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Query Latency</strong></td>\n<td>Seconds to tens of seconds</td>\n<td>Typically sub-second latency</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Custom Entity Types</strong></td>\n<td>No</td>\n<td>Yes, customizable via Pydantic models</td>\n</tr>\n<tr>\n<td><strong>Scalability</strong></td>\n<td>Moderate</td>\n<td>High, optimized for large datasets</td>\n</tr>\n</tbody>\n</table></markdown-accessiblity-table>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Graphiti is specifically designed to address the challenges of dynamic and frequently updated datasets, making it\nparticularly suitable for applications requiring real-time interaction and precise historical queries.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Installation</h2><a id=\"user-content-installation\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Installation\" href=\"#installation\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Requirements:</p>\n<ul dir=\"auto\">\n<li>Python 3.10 or higher</li>\n<li>Neo4j 5.26 / FalkorDB 1.1.2 / Amazon Neptune Database Cluster or Neptune Analytics Graph + Amazon OpenSearch\nServerless collection (serves as the full text search backend) / Kuzu 0.11.2 (<strong>deprecated</strong>, see below)</li>\n<li>OpenAI API key (Graphiti defaults to OpenAI for LLM inference and embedding)</li>\n</ul>\n<div class=\"markdown-alert markdown-alert-important\" dir=\"auto\"><p class=\"markdown-alert-title\" dir=\"auto\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-report mr-2\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"M0 1.75C0 .784.784 0 1.75 0h12.5C15.216 0 16 .784 16 1.75v9.5A1.75 1.75 0 0 1 14.25 13H8.06l-2.573 2.573A1.458 1.458 0 0 1 3 14.543V13H1.75A1.75 1.75 0 0 1 0 11.25Zm1.75-.25a.25.25 0 0 0-.25.25v9.5c0 .138.112.25.25.25h2a.75.75 0 0 1 .75.75v2.19l2.72-2.72a.749.749 0 0 1 .53-.22h6.5a.25.25 0 0 0 .25-.25v-9.5a.25.25 0 0 0-.25-.25Zm7 2.25v2.5a.75.75 0 0 1-1.5 0v-2.5a.75.75 0 0 1 1.5 0ZM9 9a1 1 0 1 1-2 0 1 1 0 0 1 2 0Z\"></path></svg>Important</p><p dir=\"auto\">Graphiti works best with LLM services that support Structured Output (such as OpenAI, Anthropic, and Gemini).\nUsing other services may result in incorrect output schemas and ingestion failures. This is particularly\nproblematic when using smaller models.</p>\n</div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Optional:</p>\n<ul dir=\"auto\">\n<li>Google Gemini, Anthropic, or Groq API key (for alternative LLM providers)</li>\n</ul>\n<div class=\"markdown-alert markdown-alert-tip\" dir=\"auto\"><p class=\"markdown-alert-title\" dir=\"auto\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-light-bulb mr-2\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"M8 1.5c-2.363 0-4 1.69-4 3.75 0 .984.424 1.625.984 2.304l.214.253c.223.264.47.556.673.848.284.411.537.896.621 1.49a.75.75 0 0 1-1.484.211c-.04-.282-.163-.547-.37-.847a8.456 8.456 0 0 0-.542-.68c-.084-.1-.173-.205-.268-.32C3.201 7.75 2.5 6.766 2.5 5.25 2.5 2.31 4.863 0 8 0s5.5 2.31 5.5 5.25c0 1.516-.701 2.5-1.328 3.259-.095.115-.184.22-.268.319-.207.245-.383.453-.541.681-.208.3-.33.565-.37.847a.751.751 0 0 1-1.485-.212c.084-.593.337-1.078.621-1.489.203-.292.45-.584.673-.848.075-.088.147-.173.213-.253.561-.679.985-1.32.985-2.304 0-2.06-1.637-3.75-4-3.75ZM5.75 12h4.5a.75.75 0 0 1 0 1.5h-4.5a.75.75 0 0 1 0-1.5ZM6 15.25a.75.75 0 0 1 .75-.75h2.5a.75.75 0 0 1 0 1.5h-2.5a.75.75 0 0 1-.75-.75Z\"></path></svg>Tip</p><p dir=\"auto\">The simplest way to install Neo4j is via <a href=\"https://neo4j.com/download/\" rel=\"nofollow\">Neo4j Desktop</a>. LLM Provider 429 Rate Limit Errors</h2><a id=\"user-content-default-to-low-concurrency-llm-provider-429-rate-limit-errors\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Default to Low Concurrency; LLM Provider 429 Rate Limit Errors\" href=\"#default-to-low-concurrency-llm-provider-429-rate-limit-errors\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Graphiti's ingestion pipelines are designed for high concurrency. By default, concurrency is set low to avoid LLM\nProvider 429 Rate Limit Errors. If you find Graphiti slow, please increase concurrency as described below.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Concurrency controlled by the <code>SEMAPHORE_LIMIT</code> environment variable. By default, <code>SEMAPHORE_LIMIT</code> is set to <code>10</code>\nconcurrent operations to help prevent <code>429</code> rate limit errors from your LLM provider. If you encounter such errors, try\nlowering this value.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">If your LLM provider allows higher throughput, you can increase <code>SEMAPHORE_LIMIT</code> to boost episode ingestion\nperformance.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Quick Start</h2><a id=\"user-content-quick-start\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Quick Start\" href=\"#quick-start\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<div class=\"markdown-alert markdown-alert-important\" dir=\"auto\"><p class=\"markdown-alert-title\" dir=\"auto\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-report mr-2\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"M0 1.75C0 .784.784 0 1.75 0h12.5C15.216 0 16 .784 16 1.75v9.5A1.75 1.75 0 0 1 14.25 13H8.06l-2.573 2.573A1.458 1.458 0 0 1 3 14.543V13H1.75A1.75 1.75 0 0 1 0 11.25Zm1.75-.25a.25.25 0 0 0-.25.25v9.5c0 .138.112.25.25.25h2a.75.75 0 0 1 .75.75v2.19l2.72-2.72a.749.749 0 0 1 .53-.22h6.5a.25.25 0 0 0 .25-.25v-9.5a.25.25 0 0 0-.25-.25Zm7 2.25v2.5a.75.75 0 0 1-1.5 0v-2.5a.75.75 0 0 1 1.5 0ZM9 9a1 1 0 1 1-2 0 1 1 0 0 1 2 0Z\"></path></svg>Important</p><p dir=\"auto\">Graphiti defaults to using OpenAI for LLM inference and embedding. Ensure that an <code>OPENAI_API_KEY</code> is set in your\nenvironment.\nSupport for Anthropic, Gemini, and Groq is available, too. Other LLM providers — both hosted OpenAI-compatible APIs\n(DeepSeek, Together, OpenRouter, …) and local servers (Ollama, vLLM, llama.cpp, LM Studio) — may be used via their\nOpenAI-compatible endpoints; see\n<a href=\"#using-graphiti-with-openai-compatible-providers-and-local-llms\">Using Graphiti with OpenAI-compatible providers and local LLMs</a>.</p>\n</div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">For a complete working example, see the <a href=\"/getzep/graphiti/blob/main/examples/quickstart/README.md\">Quickstart Example</a> in the examples directory.\nThe quickstart demonstrates:</p>\n<ol dir=\"auto\">\n<li>Connecting to a Neo4j, Amazon Neptune, FalkorDB, or Kuzu database</li>\n<li>Initializing Graphiti indices and constraints</li>\n<li>Adding episodes to the graph (both text and structured JSON)</li>\n<li>Searching for relationships (edges) using hybrid search</li>\n<li>Reranking search results using graph distance</li>\n<li>Searching for nodes using predefined search recipes</li>\n</ol>\n<p dir=\"auto\">The example is fully documented with clear explanations of each functionality and includes a comprehensive README with\nsetup instructions and next steps.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Running with Docker Compose</h3><a id=\"user-content-running-with-docker-compose\" FalkorDB Docker service and related components.</p>\n</li>\n</ul>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">MCP Server</h2><a id=\"user-content-mcp-server\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: MCP Server\" href=\"#mcp-server\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">The <code>mcp_server</code> directory contains a Model Context Protocol (MCP) server implementation for Graphiti. This server\nallows AI assistants to interact with Graphiti's context graph capabilities through the MCP protocol.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Key features of the MCP server include:</p>\n<ul dir=\"auto\">\n<li>Episode management (add, retrieve, delete)</li>\n<li>Entity management and relationship handling</li>\n<li>Semantic and hybrid search capabilities</li>\n<li>Group management for organizing related data</li>\n<li>Graph maintenance operations</li>\n</ul>\n<p dir=\"auto\">The MCP server can be deployed using Docker with Neo4j, making it easy to integrate Graphiti into your AI assistant\nworkflows.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">For detailed setup instructions and usage examples, see the <a href=\"/getzep/graphiti/blob/main/mcp_server/README.md\">MCP server README</a>.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">REST Service</h2><a id=\"user-content-rest-service\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: REST Service\" href=\"#rest-service\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">The <code>server</code> directory contains an API service for interacting with the Graphiti API. It is built using FastAPI.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Please see the <a href=\"/getzep/graphiti/blob/main/server/README.md\">server README</a> for more information.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" supported models, such as Anthropic or Voyage models, the necessary environment variables\nmust be set.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 for\ncost-effective and low-latency classification tasks. It uses the same boolean classification approach as the OpenAI\nreranker, leveraging Gemini's log probabilities feature to rank passage relevance.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Using Graphiti with OpenAI-compatible providers and local LLMs</h2><a id=\"user-content-using-graphiti-with-openai-compatible-providers-and-local-llms\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Using Graphiti with OpenAI-compatible providers and local LLMs\" href=\"#using-graphiti-with-openai-compatible-providers-and-local-llms\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Graphiti can use any OpenAI-compatible <code>/v1</code> endpoint for LLM inference via <code>OpenAIGenericClient</code> — both <strong>hosted\nproviders</strong> (DeepSeek, Together, OpenRouter, Fireworks, etc.) and <strong>local servers</strong> (Ollama, vLLM, llama.cpp, LM\nStudio). Local servers are ideal for privacy-focused applications or avoiding API costs. The example below uses Ollama;\nfor any other provider, point <code>base_url</code> at its endpoint and set the appropriate <code>api_key</code> and <code>model</code>.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\"><strong>Note:</strong> Use <code>OpenAIGenericClient</code> (not <code>OpenAIClient</code>) for these endpoints. It is optimized for local models with a\nhigher default max token limit (16K vs 8K) and handles structured outputs across compatible providers.</p>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Install the models:</p>\n<div (<code>ollama serve</code>) and that you have pulled the models you want to use.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Structured output and small models</h3><a id=\"user-content-structured-output-and-small-models\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Structured output and small models\" href=\"#structured-output-and-small-models\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Graphiti depends on structured (JSON) output for entity/edge extraction and deduplication, and works best with models\nand providers that reliably honor it (OpenAI, Anthropic, Gemini). Reliability varies across OpenAI-compatible providers and\nespecially on smaller or local models, so <code>OpenAIGenericClient</code> exposes a <code>structured_output_mode</code>:</p>\n<ul dir=\"auto\">\n<li><code>\"json_schema\"</code> (default): requests native structured output via <code>response_format</code>. Best on capable models and\nproviders that enforce the schema via constrained decoding.</li>\n<li><code>\"json_object\"</code>: requests plain-JSON mode and injects the schema into the prompt instead. Use this for\nproviders/models that don't reliably honor <code>json_schema</code> — including some local servers that accept the <code>json_schema</code>\nrequest but don't actually constrain output to it, where <code>json_object</code> can be <em>more</em> reliable.</li>\n</ul>\n<p dir=\"auto\">When using smaller or local models:</p>\n<ul dir=\"auto\">\n<li>Prefer the most capable model you can run. Very small models frequently emit JSON that doesn't match the requested\nschema, which surfaces as extraction failures.</li>\n<li>Responses wrapped in Markdown <code>```json</code> code fences are stripped automatically.</li>\n<li>Keep <code>SEMAPHORE_LIMIT</code> low (see <a href=\"#default-to-low-concurrency-llm-provider-429-rate-limit-errors\">above</a>) — local\nservers and some providers have limited concurrency.</li>\n</ul>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Documentation</h2><a id=\"user-content-documentation\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Documentation\" href=\"#documentation\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 rel=\"nofollow\">Building an agent with LangChain's LangGraph and Graphiti</a></li>\n</ul>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Telemetry</h2><a id=\"user-content-telemetry\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Telemetry\" href=\"#telemetry\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">Graphiti collects anonymous usage statistics to help us understand how the framework is being used and improve it for\neveryone. We believe transparency is important, so here's exactly what we collect and why.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">What We Collect</h3><a id=\"user-content-what-we-collect\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: What We Collect\" href=\"#what-we-collect\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">When you initialize a Graphiti instance, we collect:</p>\n<ul dir=\"auto\">\n<li><strong>Anonymous identifier</strong>: A randomly generated UUID stored locally in <code>~/.cache/graphiti/telemetry_anon_id</code></li>\n<li><strong>System information</strong>: Operating system, Python version, and system architecture</li>\n<li><strong>Graphiti version</strong>: The version you're using</li>\n<li><strong>Configuration choices</strong>:\n<ul dir=\"auto\">\n<li>LLM provider type (OpenAI, Azure, Anthropic, etc.)</li>\n<li>Database backend (Neo4j, FalkorDB, Kuzu, Amazon Neptune Database or Neptune Analytics)</li>\n<li>Embedder provider (OpenAI, Azure, Voyage, etc.)</li>\n</ul>\n</li>\n</ul>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">What We Don't Collect</h3><a id=\"user-content-what-we-dont-collect\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: What We Don't Collect\" href=\"#what-we-dont-collect\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">We are committed to protecting your privacy. We <strong>never</strong> collect:</p>\n<ul dir=\"auto\">\n<li>Personal information or identifiers</li>\n<li>API keys or credentials</li>\n<li>Your actual data, queries, or graph content</li>\n<li>IP addresses or hostnames</li>\n<li>File paths or system-specific information</li>\n<li>Any content from your episodes, nodes, or edges</li>\n</ul>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Why We Collect This Data</h3><a id=\"user-content-why-we-collect-this-data\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Why We Collect This Data\" href=\"#why-we-collect-this-data\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 4.95l-2.5 2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1-4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1 .018-1.042.751.751 0 0 1 1.042-.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l2.5-2.5a2.002 2.002 0 0 0-2.83-2.83l-1.25 1.25a.751.751 0 0 1-1.042-.018.751.751 0 0 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018 1.042.751.751 0 0 1-1.042.018 1.998 1.998 0 0 0-2.83 0l-2.5 2.5a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 0 2.83Z\"></path></svg></a></div>\n<p dir=\"auto\">This information helps us:</p>\n<ul dir=\"auto\">\n<li>Understand which configurations are most popular to prioritize support and testing</li>\n<li>Identify which LLM and database providers to focus development efforts on</li>\n<li>Track adoption patterns to guide our roadmap</li>\n<li>Ensure compatibility across different Python versions and operating systems</li>\n</ul>\n<p dir=\"auto\">By sharing this anonymous information, you help us make Graphiti better for everyone in the community.</p>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h3 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">View the dir=\"auto\">\n<li>Telemetry uses PostHog for anonymous analytics collection</li>\n<li>All telemetry operations are designed to fail silently - they will never interrupt your application or affect Graphiti\nfunctionality</li>\n<li>The anonymous ID is stored locally and is not tied to any personal information</li>\n</ul>\n<div class=\"markdown-heading\" dir=\"auto\"><h2 tabindex=\"-1\" class=\"heading-element\" dir=\"auto\">Contributing</h2><a id=\"user-content-contributing\" class=\"anchor\" aria-label=\"Permalink: Contributing\" href=\"#contributing\"><svg data-component=\"Octicon\" class=\"octicon octicon-link\" viewBox=\"0 0 16 16\" version=\"1.1\" width=\"16\" height=\"16\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><path d=\"m7.775 3.275 1.25-1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1 4.95 1-.018-1.042Zm-4.69 9.64a1.998 1.998 0 0 0 2.83 0l1.25-1.25a.751.751 0 0 1 1.042.018.751.751 0 0 1 .018 1.042l-1.25 1.25a3.5 3.5 0 1 1-4.95-4.95l2.5-2.5a3.5 3.5 0 0 1 4.95 0 .751.751 0 0 1-.018
